--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 1:**

The champion is strikingly simple: a single-state GoAwayColor from GREEN (clr=2 is green). This makes robots flee the green half of the source, pushing them toward the blue half, which apparently gives beneficial items (score-decreasing). Score: -11.93.

The second best (score -1.68) uses GoToColor WHITE (the nest area) as main state, with a transition to exploration on black floor. This is a more complex foraging approach but much worse.

The key insight: GoAwayColor from GREEN (clr=2) with score -11.93 is the champion. Robots flee green, end up collecting blue items and delivering them.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion verbatim
2. Exploit: Try GoAwayColor from GREEN with transitions to go toward nest (white) when carrying an item, or explore
3. Try GoToColor BLUE (clr=3) directly - go to the good source
4. Try 2-state machines: go away green → go to white (nest) when on black/gray
5. Try GoToColor WHITE (nest) after being on black floor

Color mapping: clr=1=red, clr=2=green, clr=3=blue, clr=4=yellow, clr=5=magenta, clr=6=cyan

The nest wall is RED (clr=1), source has BLUE (clr=3) and GREEN (clr=2) walls.

**Key insight:** GoAwayColor GREEN pushes robots toward blue source. After picking up blue item (on black floor), they need to return to nest (go toward red wall).

Let me design machines:
- 2-state: GoAwayGreen → when on black floor, GoToColor RED (nest)
- 2-state: GoToColor BLUE → when on white floor, GoAwayColor RED
- 3-state: Explore → GoToColor BLUE (on gray) → GoToColor RED (on black)

FSM: 
1. Champion (verbatim)
2-14: Variants

```
FSM: --nstates 1 --s0 9 --vel0 1.0 --cle0 6 --clr0 2
```

Now variations:

**2-state: GoAwayGreen, transition to GoToRed on black floor:**

```

```

```

```

**3-state foraging: GoAwayGreen → on black, GoToRed → on white, GoAwayGreen:**
State 0: GoAwayGreen; on black→state1; State 1: GoToRed; on white→state2; State 2: GoAwayGreen (same as 0 essentially, or exploration)

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 2:**

The champion is the 2-state machine: GoAwayGreen → on black (p=0.7) → GoToRed; GoToRed → on white (p=0.9) go back, or perceive red (p=0.3) stay. Score: -12.72.

Key observations:
1. Champion: State0=GoAwayGreen(clr=2), on BLACK→State1(GoToRed/clr=1); State1=GoToRed(clr=1), on WHITE→State0, perceive red→State0. This is a clean foraging loop: flee green → pick up blue item on black → go to red wall (nest side) → deliver on white → repeat.
2. Second best (-11.64): GoToBlue → on black → GoToRed; GoToRed → on white → GoToBlue. Similar foraging loop.
3. The simple GoAwayGreen alone scores -11.48.
4. GoToBlue alone scored 7.56 (terrible!) - robots get stuck at source.

**Key insight from champion:** The transition probability from GoAwayGreen on black floor is 0.7 (not 0.9 or 0.85). The return trigger uses BOTH white floor (p=0.9) AND perceive red (p=0.3). This combination is important.

**Exploitation strategy:**
- Vary the black floor transition probability (0.5-0.8) in champion topology
- Vary the white floor return probability (0.7-0.95)
- Vary the red perception probability (0.1-0.5)
- Try adding a gray floor transition to state1 (to catch partial returns)
- Try 3-state version with exploration state

**Exploration:**
- Try GoAwayGreen with transition on gray (going home) + GoToRed
- Try champion topology with additional neighbor-based transitions
- Try 3-state: GoAwayGreen → GoToRed → Explore on white

Let me carefully construct 14 machines:

1. Champion (verbatim)
2. Champion with p_black=0.6 (slightly lower)
3. Champion with p_black=0.8 (slightly higher)
4. Champion with p_white=0.95, p_red=0.4
5. Champion with p_white=0.8, p_red=0.2
6. Champion + gray transition in state1 (3 transitions)
7. 3-state: GoAwayGreen → GoToRed → Explore
8. Second-best variant with adjusted probabilities
9. GoAwayGreen with two transitions (black p=0.7, gray p=0.3) → GoToRed
10. Champion with added neighbor condition
11. 3-state: GoAwayGreen → GoToRed → GoToRed(exploration buffer)
12. Champion topology but GoToColor WHITE instead of RED for return
13. 2-state variant with extra transition from state0
14. 4-state exploration variant

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 3:**

The champion topology is very stable: GoAwayGreen (clr=2) → on BLACK (p=0.7) → GoToRed (clr=1); GoToRed → on WHITE (p=0.9) back, perceive RED (p=0.3) back.

Key observations:
1. The champion score is -12.72, and round 3 best was -12.53 (same topology, stochastic variation)
2. Adding a "perceive red" transition to state1 (3 transitions) tied at -12.53 - worth keeping
3. p_black=0.75 (not 0.7) scored -12.35 - slightly worse but close
4. p_black=0.7, p_white=0.8, p_red=0.2 scored -12.28 - interesting
5. GoToBlue → GoToRed scored terribly (9.59, 9.93) - confirmed bad
6. Adding gray transition to state0 (n0=2 with gray) scored -3.37 - bad (disrupts the flee behavior)
7. 3-state with exploration buffer scored -3.10 - bad

**What to try:**
- The core topology is solid: GoAwayGreen(p_black=0.7) → GoToRed(p_white=0.9, p_red=0.3)
- Fine-tune: p_black in [0.65-0.75], p_white in [0.85-0.95], p_red in [0.2-0.4]
- Try adding perceive-red transition to state0 (detect nest and stay in flee mode?)
- Try the 3-transition version of state1 more carefully
- Explore: what if we use GoAwayGreen with a gray floor transition (p=low) to trigger return earlier?
- Try GoToColor WHITE (nest wall is red, floor is white) for return
- Try 3-state: GoAwayGreen → GoToRed → GoAwayGreen (explicit loop)

**Important note:** GoToBlue is disastrous. Avoid it.

The parameter sweet spot seems to be:
- p_black (transition to return state): ~0.7
- p_white (transition back to flee): ~0.85-0.9
- p_red (perceive red, back to flee): ~0.25-0.35

Let me generate 14 machines focusing heavily on exploitation with some exploration:

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 4:**

The champion (-12.72) remains unchanged. Round 4 best was -12.19. The topology is very stable.

Key observations:
1. Best this round: p_black=0.65, 3-transition state1 (white p=0.9, red p=0.3, perceive-red p=0.5) → -12.19
2. p_black=0.68 scored -12.14
3. p_black=0.7 with 3-transition (white p=0.9, red p=0.25, perceive-red p=0.4) scored -12.12
4. Using `perceive red` (c=7, l=1) instead of `floor is red` (c=1) as second transition is WORSE (-8.80 at rank 14)
5. The champion uses c1x1=1 (perceive red color with camera) - wait, condition 1 is GRAY floor! Let me recheck.

**Re-reading the conditions:**
- C=0: floor BLACK
- C=1: floor GRAY  
- C=2: floor WHITE
- C=7: perceive a given colour with camera

So the champion has: state1 GoToRed → on WHITE (c=2, p=0.9) → back to state0, AND on GRAY (c=1, p=0.3) → back to state0.

This makes sense! When returning to nest, robot crosses gray floor then white floor. The gray transition (p=0.3) gives early return probability, and white (p=0.9) gives near-certain return at the nest.

So the "perceive red" (c=7, l=1) replacing gray (c=1) was rank 14 (-8.80) - confirmed bad.

**What to focus on:**
- The champion parameters: p_black=0.7, p_white=0.9, p_gray=0.3
- Try p_black slightly lower (0.60-0.68) since 0.65 and 0.68 did well this round
- Try varying p_gray (0.2-0.4) and p_white (0.85-0.95)
- The 3-transition version adding perceive-red (c=7, l=1) to state1 alongside the existing two transitions was competitive
- Try p_black=0.65 with slightly different p_white/p_gray combos
- Try p_black=0.60-0.63

**Exploitation (7-8 machines):**
- Champion verbatim
- p_black=0.65, 3-transition (best this round)
- p_black=0.65, 2-transition (simpler)
- p_black=0.68, 3-transition
- p_black=0.63, 2-transition
- p_black=0.67, 2-transition
- p_black=0.70, slight p_gray/p_white tweaks

**Exploration (5-6 machines):**
- Try 4-transition state1: white, gray, perceive-red, AND black (to not stay at source)
- Try p_black=0.6 with 3-transitions
- Try different p_gray values (0.15, 0.35, 0.4)
- Try GoAwayGreen with p_white transition too (state0 transitions on both black and white)

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 5:**

The champion (-12.72) still holds from round 2. The parameter landscape is now well-mapped:

- Best this round: p_black=0.67, p_white=0.9, p_gray=0.3 → -12.12
- p_black=0.68, 3-transition → -12.00
- p_black=0.65, p_gray=0.35 → -11.98
- p_black=0.65, p_gray=0.25 → -11.88

The champion has p_black=0.70, p_white=0.9, p_gray=0.3 → -12.72. This is the best ever, and nearby variants (0.67, 0.68, 0.65) score somewhat worse. The stochasticity of the simulator means we're in a noisy plateau around -12 to -12.7.

**Key insight:** We're converging to a local optimum. The topology (2-state: GoAwayGreen → GoToRed, with white and gray floor return triggers) seems near-optimal for this parameter range. We need to:

1. Continue fine-tuning around p_black=0.67-0.72, p_white=0.88-0.92, p_gray=0.25-0.35
2. Try genuinely different topologies to escape this local optimum

**What we haven't tried much:**
- GoAwayGreen with p_black transition but going to GoToColor WHITE (nest floor) instead of GoToRed
- 3-state with a dedicated "delivery" state
- Different gray transition probabilities in the return leg
- GoAwayGreen + repulsion from walls when at nest

**Exploitation (8 machines):**
- Champion verbatim
- p_black=0.70, p_gray=0.28 (small tweak)
- p_black=0.70, p_gray=0.32
- p_black=0.67, p_gray=0.28
- p_black=0.67, p_gray=0.32
- p_black=0.69, p_white=0.9, p_gray=0.3
- p_black=0.71, p_white=0.9, p_gray=0.3
- p_black=0.67, 3-transition (add perceive-red)

**Exploration (6 machines):**
- GoAwayGreen → GoToColor WHITE (instead of red) on black
- 3-state: GoAwayGreen → GoToRed → GoToRed (with exploration)
- GoAwayGreen with p_black=0.7, return via GoToColor WHITE
- Different return behavior: attraction to robots (swarm following)
- Try p_black very low (0.55) to see if more frequent returns help
- 2-state with GoToRed and BOTH gray AND white transitions at higher probability

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 6:**

A major breakthrough! The new champion scores -14.82, which is significantly better than -12.72. The winning machine is a **3-state** FSM:
- State 0: GoAwayGreen (clr=2), on BLACK (p=0.7) → State 0 (wait, destination is 0 which means... let me re-read)

Wait, with 3 states and leaving state 0, the "other states" are [1, 2], so D=0→state1, D=1→state2. So `--n0x0 0` means → state 1.

- State 0: GoAwayGreen, on BLACK (p=0.7) → State 1
- State 1: GoToRed (clr=1), on WHITE (p=0.9) → State 1 (D=1→state2? No wait)

With 3 states, leaving state 1, the "other states" are [0, 2], so D=0→state0, D=1→state2.
- State 1: GoToRed, on WHITE (p=0.9) → State 1... `--n1x0 1` means D=1→state2. So on WHITE → state2; on GRAY (p=0.3) → state2.
- State 2: GoAwayGreen (different LED), on BLACK (p=0.7) → state0 (D=0→state0); on GRAY (p=0.4) → state1 (D=1→state1)

So the structure is: GoAwayGreen → GoToRed → GoAwayGreen(variant) → loop. The third state (GoAwayGreen with magenta LED, clr=2) transitions on black→state0 and gray→state1.

This is essentially: two GoAwayGreen states that alternate, with GoToRed in between. The second GoAwayGreen has a gray transition that can re-trigger the return trip.

**Key insight:** Having a second GoAwayGreen state (state2) that can return to GoToRed when on gray floor creates a more robust behavior - if the robot is still crossing gray when it should be at the source, it gets another chance to return.

**Exploitation strategy:**
- Keep champion verbatim
- Vary parameters of champion: p_black in state0/state2, p_gray in state2, p_white in state1
- Try state2 with only one transition (just black→state0)
- Try different p_gray for state2 (0.3, 0.35, 0.45)
- Try swapping state2 to GoAwayRed instead of GoAwayGreen

**Exploration:**
- 4-state version extending the champion
- Try state2 as Exploration instead of GoAwayGreen
- Try state2 as GoToColor BLUE (go to source directly)
- Try 3-state with state2 having more transitions

Let me construct 13 machines:

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 8:**

The champion (-15.51) holds from round 7. Round 8 best was -14.76 - none beat the champion. We're in a noisy plateau around -14 to -15.5.

**Key observations:**
- All round 8 machines scored between -14.14 and -14.76, all worse than champion -15.51
- The champion parameters (p_black_s0=0.7, p_white_s1=0.9, p_gray_s1=0.3, p_black_s2=0.7, p_gray_s2=0.4) appear to be near-optimal
- Small variations around champion don't reliably improve it - just noise
- p_gray_s1=0.29 was best this round (-14.76) but still worse than champion
- Adding perceive-red to state2 (-14.48) slightly worse
- p_black_s2=0.72/0.73 worse than 0.7
- The champion has been tested multiple times (rounds 7 and 8) and scored -15.51 and -14.26 - high variance!

**The issue is noise:** The simulator is stochastic. The champion scored -15.51 once but -14.26 in another run. We need to keep exploring to find a configuration that consistently scores lower.

**Strategy for round 9:**
1. Keep champion verbatim (elitism)
2. Re-run champion topology with slight variations to get more samples
3. Try genuinely different explorations we haven't tried yet:
   - What if state0 has TWO transitions (black AND something else)?
   - What if state1 transitions to state0 instead of state2 on some conditions?
   - Try state2 GoAwayColor clr=3 (green=2 was already tried; try something else)
   - Try a 4-state version where state3 is GoToRed as a dedicated delivery state

**Important insight:** The destination numbering with 3 states. State2 transitions: n2x0 0 → state0 (the other states from state2's perspective are [0,1], D=0→state0, D=1→state1). This is correct in champion.

Let me try:
- Champion (verbatim)
- Very close variants of champion
- Try state2 as GoAwayColor clr=1 (go away red = move away from nest, toward source)
- Try 4-state with explicit looping
- Try different state1 destinations (what if on gray it goes to state0 instead of state2?)

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 9:**

New champion: -16.22! The winning machine added a **3rd transition to state2**: on WHITE floor (p=0.6) → state0. 

Champion state2 now has:
- on BLACK (p=0.7) → state0 (go back to flee-green phase)
- on GRAY (p=0.4) → state1 (go back to return-to-nest phase)  
- on WHITE (p=0.6) → state0 (if reached nest, restart flee-green)

This makes state2 (the second GoAwayGreen) very responsive: if it sees black (at source) → restart; if gray (midway) → return to nest; if white (at nest) → restart flee.

**Key observations:**
- Champion: 3-transition state2 with p_white=0.6 → -16.22 (big jump!)
- p_gray_s1=0.33 → -15.22
- p_gray_s1=0.27 → -15.18
- Swapping state1 gray destination to state0 → -12.22 (confirmed: gray in state1 must go to state2)
- Changing state2 gray condition to floor-black (c=0) → -11.90 (confirmed: c2x1=1 must be gray)
- Adding random transition to state0 → -3.44 (bad)

**Exploitation:**
- Champion verbatim
- Vary p_white in state2 (0.5, 0.55, 0.65, 0.7)
- Vary p_black in state2 (0.65, 0.68, 0.72)
- Vary p_gray in state2 (0.35, 0.38, 0.42, 0.45)
- Combine: best p_white with variations in other params
- Try 4-transition state2 (add perceive-red)
- Try state1 with 3 transitions

**Exploration:**
- Try p_white_s2=0.5 or 0.7 with other variations
- Try state2 with 4 transitions
- Try varying p_black_s0

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

The arena contains a nest area at one end, with a WHITE floor, and a source area at the opposite end, with a BLACK floor. The floor in between is GRAY. The wall on the nest side is RED.

The source area is divided into two halves. The wall blocks behind one half are BLUE and those behind the other half are GREEN.

A robot carries at most one item. Entering a half of the source gives the robot an item of that half's type, replacing any item it was already carrying. Entering the nest with an item delivers it: an item from one half decreases the score by 1, an item from the other half increases it by 1.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
